86:"$Sreact.suspense"
88:I[97367,["/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0k3m1mxwvr8x..js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/07qi2okp-.yp9.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0acfy-3mj9oa_.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0n4z.7yu76je-.js"],"ViewportBoundary"]
8a:I[97367,["/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0k3m1mxwvr8x..js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/07qi2okp-.yp9.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0acfy-3mj9oa_.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0n4z.7yu76je-.js"],"MetadataBoundary"]
15:T417,The paper compares standard pretraining, SPT, with parallel pretraining, PPT. In SPT, source documents and their translations are sampled as independent examples. In PPT, each document is concatenated with its translation inside the same 2,048-token training context. Each segment is prefixed with a language label such as English: or French:, and the language order is alternated so that both translation directions receive cross-lingual context. The objective remains ordinary autoregressive next-token prediction, with loss on both languages and no additional contrastive or translation-specific objective. The key mechanism is that when predicting the later segment, the model can attend to a semantically corresponding document in another language. The paper then evaluates two-stage schedules. PPT→SPT first builds cross-lingual use of parallel context and then adapts the model back to independently sampled documents. SPT→PPT tests the reverse order and helps isolate the importance of ending training on the target usage distribution.16:T444,The authors train 1.3B dense Llama 2-style models from scratch on TransWebEdu for English–French and English–Swahili. The language pairs cover a higher-resource and a lower-resource case. Single-stage SPT and PPT runs are trained for roughly 200B tokens. The two-stage comparisons use matched total budgets, including a reported 120B-token setting with a 20B-token second stage, and compare SPT-only, PPT-only, PPT→SPT, and SPT→PPT. PPT alone does not consistently improve general language understanding. Behavioral analysis shows that PPT models benefit more from cross-lingual context, but this advantage is not fully exercised by standard single-language tasks. PPT→SPT improves average general language understanding in both language settings and gives the best translation performance. Relative to SPT-only, BLEU improves from 19.25 to 34.03 on English–French, a 77% relative gain, and from 9.07 to 13.18 on English–Swahili, a 45% relative gain. Continued SPT improves monolingual language modeling while retaining an advantage in using cross-lingual context over SPT-only.17:T8fd,方法核心是把 teacher 与 student 的预测重写到一个 common support 上。做法分三步。

第一步是序列对齐。作者先用字符级 offset 和 decode 后文本一致性检查，找出 student token 序列与 teacher token 序列中覆盖同一文本子串的 chunk 对齐关系。这样对齐的不是 token id，而是“相同文本跨度”。

第二步是 common-support construction。对每个对齐 chunk，构造一组“文本等价 continuation event”。如果是 1-to-1 chunk，事件可以是两边词表里 decode 成同一字符串的 token 对；如果是 1-to-many 或 many-to-1，事件就变成“一边一个 token，对应另一边一个 token sequence”。关键点在于，sequence event 的概率不是额外建模，而是直接用自回归链式法则，把 chunk 内各步条件概率连乘得到。文中举的例子是 teacher 一个 token“120”对应 student 三个 token“[1][2][0]”；固定前缀 [1][2] 后，可以把 120↔[1][2][0]、121↔[1][2][1] 等都作为显式事件。这样做比 ALM 只保留 observed chunk 更细，因为它把 alternative continuations 也展开到了共同支持集里。

第三步是 residual normalization + uniform matching。显式列出的共同事件之外，其余概率质量都并入一个 residual event。于是 teacher 和 student 都得到定义在同一 support 上的归一化分布，可以统一用一个分布匹配目标训练，不再需要像 X-Token 那样按 tokenizer 覆盖率切换 P-KL/H-KL。这个设计的取舍很清楚：它不追求枚举全部跨词表续写，而是在可计算的 support 上尽量保留结构，再用 residual 保证概率闭合。

从全文看，真正的创新不只是“加了一个 residual”，而是把跨 tokenizer 对齐从 token identity matching 改成 text-equivalent event matching，并且把 one-to-many / many-to-one 情况纳入同一个目标。这样统一后，训练接口更像标准 logits KD，工程上更容易接到现有蒸馏流水线里。

另一个实用点是 top-k distillation。作者发现只取 teacher top-16 或 top-128 token 来构造 support，平均性能只小幅下降，但训练步时显著降低。这说明 CSD 的收益主要来自 teacher 分布头部的结构信息，而不是完整长尾。18:T878,CSD builds a common support of text-equivalent continuation events and matches teacher and student on that support.

Step 1 is sequence alignment. The method aligns student and teacher token ranges that decode to the same text span, using character offsets plus decoded-text consistency checks. The alignment target is therefore shared text, not shared token ids.

Step 2 is common-support construction. For each aligned chunk, CSD defines explicit events corresponding to equivalent textual continuations. In 1-to-1 cases, an event can be a pair of tokens from the two vocabularies that decode to the same string. In 1-to-many or many-to-1 cases, an event becomes a token on one side paired with a token sequence on the other side. The probability of a sequence event is computed directly from the model’s autoregressive conditionals via the chain rule. The example in the paper is a teacher token like “120” aligned with a student sequence “[1][2][0]”; by fixing the within-chunk prefix “[1][2]”, one can enumerate events such as 120↔[1][2][0], 121↔[1][2][1], and so on. This is the key difference from ALM: CSD does not only keep the observed chunk and a catch-all complement, but explicitly represents alternative continuations when they can be mapped onto shared textual outcomes.

Step 3 is residual normalization and uniform matching. Any probability mass outside the selected explicit support is assigned to a residual event. This gives both teacher and student normalized distributions over the same event set, so a single matching objective can be used across chunk types and tokenizer pairs. That removes the need for pair-specific loss selection such as X-Token’s P-KL versus H-KL.

The real contribution is not merely adding a residual bucket. It is the move from token-identity matching to text-equivalent event matching, while covering 1-to-1, 1-to-many, and many-to-1 cases within one formulation. The paper also shows a useful efficiency angle: restricting the teacher side to top-k logits preserves most of the gain, implying that the head of the teacher distribution carries most of the transferable structure for this objective.19:T6a4,The evaluation spans multiple cross-family teacher-student settings. Students include Llama-3.2-1B, Gemma3-1B-PT, and larger Llama-3.1-8B base and instruct models. Teachers include Qwen3-4B and Phi-4-mini. The setup is explicitly cross-tokenizer and cross-family rather than same-architecture distillation.

For 1B-scale students, CSD achieves the best average accuracy across four settings. It improves over X-Token by 0.66 to 5.38 points and over ALM by 1.33 to 3.30 points, while also outperforming continued pretraining and same-tokenizer KD. That is an important result because it isolates the gain to the distillation objective rather than to longer training or tokenizer similarity.

At larger scale, the gains remain stable. For Llama-3.1-8B base, CSD beats continued pretraining by 2.17 to 4.43 points and ALM by 2.61 to 4.23 points across two teachers. For Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct, it improves over continued training and ALM by 3.90 and 1.60 points respectively, suggesting the method still helps after instruction tuning.

The efficiency study is also useful. In the Qwen-to-Llama setting, using only the teacher top-16 or top-128 logits reduces the five-task average by just 1.74% or 0.74% relative to full logits, while substantially lowering step time. That makes the method more realistic for large-scale KD where teacher forward cost and logits transfer are bottlenecks.

The ablation logic supports three claims: a uniform objective is more robust than tokenizer-pair-specific heuristics, explicit alternative continuations retain information that ALM discards, and support truncation is a favorable trade-off. The gains appear consistent rather than tied to one lucky tokenizer pair.1a:T467,For a quick but useful read, focus on three parts.

First, read the problem setup in the introduction, especially the critique of GOLD, X-Token, and ALM. That section makes clear why CSD is not just another loss variant but a different way to define comparable prediction events.

Second, read the main CSD method section carefully, with attention to how events are constructed for different chunk shapes. The most important point is how 1-to-many and many-to-1 cases are handled with autoregressive chain-rule probabilities, and why the residual event preserves normalization without collapsing everything back into ALM.

Third, inspect the main result tables and the top-k experiments. The former tells you whether the method is stable across tokenizer pairs; the latter tells you whether it is deployable in a real distillation pipeline.

If time is limited, 30 to 45 minutes is enough to understand the core idea and empirical message. If reading more deeply, check the appendix algorithm and the handling of accepted many-to-many chunks, since that is where implementation robustness and edge-case behavior become clearer.1b:T496,FlashNext-RL is built on prime-rl for training and orchestration, with vLLM used for SWE rollout generation. The target model has 48 layers and 125B parameters with about 6B activated parameters per token, plus a 51B frozen n-gram table and a 4B MTP head. The training setup freezes the first two layers, the n-gram table, the QSA indexer, and the MTP head. On the inference side, the system uses vLLM 0.31 with BF16 weights and BF16 KV cache, two TP=4 engines per node, expert parallelism, and MTP speculative decoding. A key memory observation is that TP-only introduces padding waste for the MoE experts: a 640-wide expert split across TP=4 gives 160 columns per rank, padded to 256, causing 1.6x expansion. Expert parallelism keeps experts whole and reduces memory pressure. On the training side, the system combines BF16 training, CPU offload, streaming Muon, expert parallelism, context parallelism, and custom kernels for QSA sparse grouped-query attention backward, MoE with intermediate size 640, gated residual mixing, and per-layer embeddings. The end-to-end loop also includes weight-transfer and checkpoint optimizations to fit within a 2 TB host-memory budget.1c:T72d,The paper rewrites Muon as an explicit metric/preconditioning method and then analyzes the statistics of that preconditioner at initialization.

1) Preconditioner form of orthogonalization: for a matrix parameter W∈R^{m×n}, Muon replaces the momentum/gradient matrix V with its orthogonal polar factor D=(V V^T)^(-1/2)V (computed via a fixed-point polar iteration / thin SVD). Vectorizing yields vec(D)=((V V^T)^(-1/2)⊗I_n)vec(V), i.e., Muon performs descent in a Kronecker-structured metric determined by P=V V^T (row-space Gram).

2) Structural alignment target: prior work reports that neural-network layer Hessians are row-block diagonally dominant when blocks are indexed by output neurons (rows of W). Muon’s preconditioner also only mixes along rows (left-multiplication by (V V^T)^(-1/2)), so if P is close to diagonal, Muon inherits the same row-block structure as the Hessian.

3) Main theory: at initialization (V0=G0), prove for four canonical models—symmetric matrix factorization, deep linear networks, two-layer ReLU networks, and single-layer linear attention—that the diagonal of E[G G^T] outgrows the off-diagonal as hidden dimensions increase, with explicit width-dependent rates (Theorems 1–4, summarized in Table 1). Technically, this is a correlation decay result: gradient rows corresponding to different output neurons become weakly correlated due to averaging in the wide limit.

4) Empirical support: simulations reproduce the predicted scaling trends; initialization-time measurements on real LLMs (Qwen3.5, Llama-3.2, Granite-4.0) show stronger diagonal dominance with larger width.

Key difference from earlier analyses: instead of generic optimization assumptions, the argument is tied to neural-network-specific Hessian structure plus width-induced diagonalization of the gradient Gram matrix.1d:T4e7,The authors model long-context performance using three predictors: non-embedding parameter count N, short-context pretraining tokens Ds, and long-context continual-pretraining tokens Dl. LongPPL is used as the proxy metric. The proposed empirical law is an additive power law with an offset, where model size, short-context data, and long-context data each contribute separate power-law terms. This separation is important because LCCP starts from a pretrained model; its behavior is not equivalent to training from scratch with a single combined token budget. For monitoring, the paper compares linear-decay and constant-learning-rate trajectories. Linear decay gives stable endpoint performance but requires the total budget to be fixed in advance. A constant learning rate makes cumulative Dl a natural progress coordinate, but raw checkpoints are noisy and underperform budget-matched linear-decay endpoints. The Constant-Last4 protocol evaluates the average of the four most recent consecutive checkpoints. This reduces checkpoint noise and makes constant-LR observations closer to linear-decay performance. With N and Ds fixed, the law reduces to a one-dimensional power law in Dl, allowing later LongPPL to be predicted from early LCCP checkpoints.1e:T472,The scaling-law fit uses 109 configurations across five model scales, varying short-context and long-context token budgets under a linear-decay schedule. The fitted law reproduces observed trends and extrapolates to held-out model scales within the tested budget regime. The monitoring experiments apply Constant-Last4 on constant-LR LCCP trajectories and show that the same law remains usable; the paper reports extrapolation from smaller settings to an approximately 100B-token LCCP run for a 1.22B model. Capability validation is done along a 32K LCCP trajectory with RULER, where aggregate performance keeps improving with more long-context tokens and shows no clear saturation in the evaluated range. The authors also test whether the gains survive later stages: 128K context extension and supervised fine-tuning are applied with identical downstream procedures, and the benefits from larger LCCP budgets remain visible on long-context and agentic benchmarks. The provided text does not expose the exact benchmark scores or percentage gains, so the safest reading is about trend predictability rather than a universal optimal budget.1f:T433,The main empirical results are consistent and useful. More relevant data improves in-corpus performance, with large gains in Minecraft as the data budget grows. Mixture diversity improves transfer to related worlds but gives limited benefit on distant worlds, suggesting that virtual worlds share less stable structure than physical environments. Larger models are more data-efficient in the early phase where they learn self-control and action primitives, but under single-game training they can generalize worse. The authors describe a two-phase dynamic: an early interface phase for basic control and action primitives, followed by a world phase where additional gains are tied more closely to trained or related environments. An empirical scaling law over validation loss, data volume, mixture diversity, and model size reaches R²=0.982 on Minecraft loss and R²=0.995 on in-corpus loss. The models are also reported to be competitive with Minecraft specialists and general foundation models in 3D worlds, and up to 5× better than frontier models in real-time settings.20:Taa6,方法由三个关键部件组成。

第一，训练目标。作者采用 uniform token replacement 而不是 mask corruption。也就是每个位置以概率 1-α_t 被替换成词表中的均匀随机 token，而不是替换成特殊 [MASK]。这样得到的输入始终是一份“完整草稿”，错误 token 没有显式标记，模型必须同时学会两件事：当当前位置本来就对时要保留，当它错时要修复。训练时在所有位置上监督 clean-token posterior，等价于让 denoiser 在完整但带噪的草稿上做全位置重建。

第二，单次前向的上下文评估。直接拿 denoiser 对当前 token 的概率给草稿打分会有偏差，因为在 uniform corruption 信道下，观察到 token x_i 本身会显著提高 x_i 为真的后验概率，即便把该位置拿掉后，周围上下文其实更支持另一个 token。作者用已知的 corruption process 做 Bayes 校正，把“观察到自身 token 带来的证据”除掉，再重归一化，恢复出该位置的 cavity conditional，也就是 p(x_i | c, z_{-i}, t) 这一类“给定条件和其他 noisy token，但不看自己”的分布。然后把草稿每个位置真实 token 的负对数 cavity conditional 相加，得到 cavity energy。这个量不是精确联合似然，而是一个噪声平滑后的 pseudo-likelihood 型能量分数，但足够用于候选草稿之间的比较。

第三，推理算法 propose-read-retain。推理不是标准 diffusion ancestral sampling，也不是每轮完全重启。流程是：保留当前最优草稿；对其做部分 re-noise，避免陷入局部最优，同时保留部分结构；用同一个 denoiser 对所有位置并行预测 clean token，直接形成 candidate draft；再用一次新的前向、在固定 read noise level 下计算 candidate 的 cavity energy；若能量更低则接受，否则保留旧草稿。所有 proposal pass 和 scoring pass 都计入固定预算，最后返回最低能量的草稿。这里有两个设计很关键：一是“提案”和“评估”分开，避免用生成时的同一组 logits 给自己背书；二是 retain best draft，而不是每轮独立采样，这使得搜索更像局部修订而不是盲目重启。

和已有方法相比，差异主要在三点。相对 MDLM/UDLM 一类离散 diffusion，作者不依赖 reverse transition 逐步采样，而是把 denoiser 的 clean-token 预测直接当作修订操作。相对普通 non-autoregressive refinement，作者补上了一个有理论依据的整稿评分机制，而不是默认并行替换一定更好。相对 energy-based LM，这里的能量不是单独训练的打分器，而是从 denoiser 的后验结构中解析出来的。21:T477,For pretraining and alternative language-modeling paradigms, the contribution is meaningful in three ways. First, it moves diffusion-style language modeling away from schedule-driven ancestral denoising toward a draft-refinement loop where the denoiser acts as both editor and evaluator. Second, it introduces cavity energy, which turns a denoiser trained under uniform corruption into a contextual whole-draft scoring mechanism resembling noise-smoothed pseudo-likelihood. That is relevant beyond this paper: masked LM, any-order LM, energy-based LM, and draft-based reasoning all face the same issue of scoring partially self-supported token configurations. Third, it identifies and fixes a subtle but important failure mode: denoiser confidence on an observed token mixes contextual support with evidence from seeing that token itself. If that self-token evidence is not removed, the model can prefer internally stable but wrong drafts. This is not a drop-in replacement for mainstream large-scale AR pretraining, but it is a serious step in the line of work that tries to challenge next-token decoding with alternative generation dynamics.22:T9f4,The method has three main components.

1) Training under uniform corruption. Instead of mask corruption, each token is independently replaced with a uniformly sampled vocabulary token with probability 1-α_t. The noisy input is therefore always a complete draft: every position contains an ordinary token, and wrong positions are unmarked. The denoiser is trained to reconstruct the clean sequence at every position. This forces it to learn both preservation and repair: keep a token when it is already supported, change it when the surrounding draft suggests it is wrong.

2) One-pass contextual evaluation via cavity correction. A naive score based on the denoiser probability of the current token is biased, because under the known corruption channel, observing token x_i itself is strong evidence in favor of x_i, even if the rest of the draft would support another token. The paper derives a correction that divides out this self-token likelihood and renormalizes, recovering the cavity conditional: the distribution of x_i given the condition and all other noisy draft tokens, but not the observed token at position i itself. Summing the negative log cavity probabilities assigned to the draft tokens yields cavity energy. This is not an exact joint likelihood; it is a learned, noise-smoothed pseudo-likelihood-style energy. But it is enough to rank candidate drafts.

3) Propose-read-retain inference. Inference starts from random tokens. At each round, the current retained draft is partially re-noised, then the same denoiser predicts clean tokens in parallel to form a candidate draft directly, without a diffusion reverse transition. A fresh forward pass at a fixed read level computes the candidate's cavity energy. The candidate is accepted only if its energy is lower; otherwise the retained draft stays. Proposal passes and scoring passes both count toward a fixed inference budget, and the final output is the lowest-energy draft seen so far.

The design differences from prior work are important. Compared with MDLM/UDLM-style discrete diffusion, the model does not rely on schedule-specific reverse transitions for generation; clean-token predictions are used directly as edits. Compared with generic non-autoregressive refinement, the paper adds a principled whole-draft evaluation rule instead of assuming that parallel local improvements compose globally. Compared with energy-based LM, the energy is not learned by a separate scorer; it is extracted from the denoiser's posterior structure using the known corruption process.23:T686,Experiments focus on synthetic reasoning tasks that require global consistency rather than fluent local continuation: Countdown, Sudoku-Extreme, and Maze-Hard. The model is small, only 6M parameters, which makes the comparison about generation dynamics rather than scale.

Main results: DRLM reaches 87.6% on Countdown-4, 44.4% on Countdown-5, 93.9% on Sudoku-Extreme, and 73.0% on Maze-Hard. Relative to the strongest diffusion baseline evaluated in the paper, the gains are +14.3, +34.1, +28.4, and +33.7 percentage points respectively. The size of the gains suggests that the benefit is not a minor decoding tweak; it comes from the proposal-and-evaluation loop itself.

The ablations are especially informative. Retaining the best draft matters: restarting independently or failing to preserve the lowest-energy draft hurts performance, which shows that the search accumulates structured improvements over time. Partial re-noising is better than either freezing the draft too aggressively or restarting from scratch, indicating a useful balance between preserving global structure and escaping local optima. Self-token correction is critical: without it, the model overvalues drafts whose tokens are merely self-supported by observation under the corruption channel. Separating proposal from evaluation with a fresh read pass also helps, meaning the logits used to generate a candidate should not be trusted as an unbiased score of that same candidate.

The largest gain appears on Countdown-5, which is consistent with the paper's claim: the harder the task depends on global compatibility among many local choices, the more valuable whole-draft evaluation becomes.24:T599,Read the paper in three passes.

First, focus on the cavity derivation. The key question is not whether every equation is elegant, but why denoiser confidence on an observed token is biased in the first place, how the known corruption channel lets the authors remove that bias, and why the resulting quantity behaves like a pseudo-likelihood term. That is the conceptual center of the paper.

Second, study the propose-read-retain algorithm and its pseudocode. Pay attention to the separation between proposal noise and read level, and to the use of a fresh scoring pass. This is what turns the method into energy-controlled local search rather than ordinary diffusion decoding.

Third, spend time on the ablations. The most important comparisons are: with vs. without self-token correction, with vs. without best-draft retention, and with partial re-noising vs. alternative restart strategies. Those three ablations map almost exactly to the three pillars of the method.

If reading time is limited, the introduction, the cavity scoring section, the algorithm box, and the main ablation table are enough to capture the paper's substance. The main open question worth probing further is scalability: the method is convincing on 6M models and structured synthetic tasks, but it remains unclear how stable cavity-energy ranking will be for long natural-language reasoning and whether uniform corruption remains the right channel there.25:T5f0,Setup: continuous dLLMs operate entirely in embedding space during the diffusion/flow trajectory. A denoising head is trained with regression (MSE) to predict clean embeddings (x-prediction), and a decoding head maps embeddings to vocabulary only at the final step.

Mechanism argument:
1) Measure tail collapse by comparing generated word rates to held-out human text from the same distribution, with rarity defined by corpus frequency (not model probability).
2) Explain collapse via mean-seeking regression: when the conditional distribution over tokens is multi-modal (rare token vs common competitor), regression pulls the state toward the conditional mean, effectively favoring the common mode. By the time the final decoding head is applied, each position has only ~1.02–1.05 “effective candidates,” so sampler knobs cannot recover the tail.

GRAFT (training-free) restores a discrete sampling interface mid-generation:
- During denoising, it periodically reads the decoding head to obtain a token distribution.
- It identifies rare tokens that still have substantial probability but are being overtaken by more common competitors.
- It restarts generation from noise while hard-fixing (committing) those rare tokens from the first step, letting the rest of the sequence adapt around them.
- Repeating this read-and-restart accumulates committed rare words; a final generation produces the output.
Subword-level constraints and an anti-repetition correction reduce artifacts introduced by hard token fixing.26:T44e,HMDLM augments masked discrete diffusion with a discrete hierarchy variable h_i for each position.
- Forward diffusion: not only corrupts tokens x toward [MASK], but also diffuses hierarchy states toward an unknown/masked state, so the reverse process must recover structure.
- Reverse denoising is factorized into two stages:
  1) Predict hierarchy for masked positions: p(h | x_t).
  2) Predict tokens conditioned on hierarchy: p(x_0 | x_t, h).
  This encourages the model to infer “which level this position belongs to” before committing to token identities.
- Level-specific schedules: each hierarchy level has its own noise/unmasking schedule, implementing “resolve higher-level positions earlier” at the process level rather than via ad-hoc decoding.
- Training: derives a hierarchy-aware negative ELBO (NELBO) for the joint diffusion over tokens and hierarchy.
- Position-hierarchy vs prior work: contrasts with anchor-based diffusion (importance depends on token identity) and planning-aware decoding (often inference-time only); HMDLM bakes hierarchy into both training and generation.27:T7fe,The paper is a controlled empirical study built around five pretraining variants. The starting point is an 86M-parameter model trained on four languages in four scripts. From there, mpixel 8l adds four more languages in an already seen script, allowing the authors to test language diversity while holding script familiarity fixed. mpixel 8s adds four languages in previously unseen scripts, testing script diversity. mpixel base combines both expansions and reaches sixteen languages. mpixel large scales capacity from 86M to 307M to test whether larger models mitigate multilingual interference.

The data comes from FineWeb and multilingual FineWeb2. A notable design choice is to allocate equal numbers of image patches per language rather than equal numbers of documents or text tokens. That is the right unit for a pixel LM and makes compute more comparable across scripts. Each language receives roughly 7.5B image patches, with a maximum sequence length of 512 patches.

A technically important part is the multilingual rendering strategy. The paper does not use one universal rendering rule. It defaults to grapheme-aware bigrams to avoid splitting diacritics from base characters. For CJK, where wide characters make bigrams inefficient, it renders one character per patch. For Arabic and Hebrew, where bigram rendering can break ligatures and visual order can distort word structure, it uses word-level rendering in logical order. This is partly an engineering contribution, but for pixel LMs it directly affects redundancy, effective context length, and cross-lingual sharing.

The analysis goes beyond downstream scores. The authors use ABX discrimination to study cross-lingual alignment and probing to locate sentence-level and word-level information across layers. One interesting finding is that the larger 307M model appears to reserve more layers for visual processing, suggesting that extra capacity first relieves front-end representational pressure rather than immediately translating into stronger language-level alignment.28:T6c2,The evaluation covers five tasks and 83 languages: sentence classification, dependency parsing, named entity recognition, natural language inference, and English GLUE as a high-resource reference. Results are broken down into pretraining languages, unseen languages in seen scripts, and languages in unseen scripts. That split is essential because the average can hide very different transfer regimes.

There are four main empirical conclusions. First, scaling acts locally. Adding more languages within a known script mostly helps those added languages and nearby languages in the same script regime. Adding new scripts mostly helps the newly covered scripts. Second, transfer to unseen scripts emerges with multilingual pretraining, but additional scaling in language count, script count, or model size does not keep improving it. That suggests a threshold effect from shared visual structure rather than a smooth scaling law. Third, pixel LMs also suffer from the curse of multilinguality: as more languages are added, performance on the shared original languages drops, and larger capacity partially mitigates that drop. Fourth, on low-resource languages that are typologically distant from XLM-R’s pretraining languages, mpixel outperforms subword and character baselines, which supports the case for pixel-based modeling on the long tail.

The representation analysis adds nuance. Cross-lingual alignment weakens as more languages are added, yet generalization can still occur through shared script structure. The larger model also appears to allocate more depth to visual processing. The paper is less about chasing a single benchmark score and more about answering the four scaling questions with controlled comparisons.29:T5da,The method has three components. First, dynamic leader selection. For each token, among the Top-K selected experts, one expert is sampled as the leader according to normalized routing scores rather than deterministically taking the top-scoring expert. This prevents one expert from always dominating the unmodified update path. Second, gradient accumulation. Each expert stores a recent history of output gradients as an orthonormal basis using Gram-Schmidt, forming a compact subspace memory of past update directions. Third, gradient projection. The leader keeps its original task gradient. Each follower removes the component of its output gradient that lies in the leader’s accumulated gradient subspace, keeping only the orthogonal component. As a result, co-routed experts processing the same token are discouraged from moving in highly redundant directions.

This differs cleanly from prior work. It does not redesign the router, does not require extra forward or backward passes, and does not enforce diversity through an auxiliary loss in parameter space or representation space. It acts directly on expert output gradients that already exist in the standard training pipeline. In that sense, it is a backward-time intervention rather than a new objective. The extra cost comes from maintaining per-expert gradient bases and performing projections. The exposition is written for Top-2 routing, but the idea extends naturally to Top-K by choosing one leader and de-overlapping the others.2a:T4bd,From the visible full-text portion, the experimental story is likely organized around three axes: downstream task quality, degree of expert specialization, and compatibility with existing specialization methods. The introduction explicitly positions GaP-MoE against router-based methods, auxiliary-loss methods, and recent gradient-regulation methods, so the experiments likely compare against representatives from those families and show improved expert diversity without extra forward/backward cost.

What can be stated with confidence from the available text is limited. The claimed outcome is that explicit gradient orthogonalization reduces representation collapse and remains compatible with existing router architectures and auxiliary losses. The most important ablations should include: dynamic leader sampling versus deterministic leader choice, the effect of gradient memory size or basis rank, projection with and without accumulation, and behavior under different Top-K settings. The provided text does not include concrete model sizes, dataset sizes, or exact gains, so any numeric summary would be speculative. Those details need to be checked directly in the experiment section and appendix tables.2b:T48e,For a skim, focus on two questions: where exactly does the method intervene, and why is projection preferable to adding an orthogonality loss. Start with the overview figure and Algorithm 1 to understand the leader/follower split, gradient accumulation, and projection pipeline. Then read the part that clarifies the intervention happens on expert output gradients rather than parameter gradients; that is the key to relating this work to PCGrad-style methods, orthogonal regularization, and router balancing.

In the experiments, prioritize three kinds of evidence: whether task performance is preserved or improved, whether expert similarity or utilization metrics actually improve, and what the runtime or memory overhead looks like. If time is limited, the main results table, specialization visualizations, and core ablations should be enough for a first-pass judgment in about 20–30 minutes. For deeper reading, the interesting questions are whether the stored gradient subspace becomes stale, whether stochastic leader selection adds harmful noise, and how this interacts with load balancing and capacity constraints in large-scale language MoE pretraining.2c:T816,PLRM 的核心是一个变分式 latent reasoning 框架。论文先明确了传统 CoT-SFT 的三个优点：一是 next-token 预测天然是随机的，可以采样多条轨迹；二是每个中间 token 都提供密集监督；三是 teacher forcing 允许并行训练。作者认为，很多 latent reasoning 方法虽然减少了语言冗余，却丢掉了其中至少一部分，尤其是随机性和分布建模能力。

为此，PLRM 不再把 latent step 当成一个确定向量序列，而是把每一步 latent reasoning state 建模成条件分布。训练时，posterior 网络从问题 x 和 verbalized CoT 中构造目标 latent distribution；prior 网络只看问题条件，去预测这些 latent 分布。这个训练目标是 JEPA 风格的 predictive objective：不是逐 token 重建文本，而是在潜空间里让 prior 对齐 posterior 所定义的目标表示。这样做有两个直接好处。第一，它鼓励 latent state 保留对求解有用的核心推理信息，而不是去拟合语言表面的措辞差异。第二，它保留了 teacher-forced、可并行的训练结构，因为 posterior 可以从完整 CoT 提供监督信号。

论文进一步指出，同一道题可能对应多条有效 CoT，因此目标 latent 分布天然可能是多峰的。如果只用单高斯 prior，容易把多条推理路径压成一个平均态，既不利于采样，也不利于表达不确定性。为此作者引入 mixture-based prior，用混合分布去拟合多模态 latent target，而不是把所有轨迹揉成一个 unimodal Gaussian。这个设计是全文最关键的技术点：它不是简单给 latent 加噪声，而是明确把 reasoning trajectory 当作分布对象来学。

从方法定位看，PLRM 与 Coconut、CODI、Sim-CoT 这类确定性 latent reasoning 的差别，在于它把“潜空间推理”从 sequence compression 推向了 distribution modeling；与一般随机 latent model 的差别，在于它借助 posterior-prior 配对和 JEPA 式目标，试图缓解训练不稳与 posterior collapse。2d:T85b,PLRM is a variational latent reasoning framework. The paper starts by identifying three useful properties of standard CoT-SFT: next-token prediction is stochastic and supports trajectory sampling; every intermediate token provides dense supervision; and teacher forcing enables parallel training. The authors argue that many latent reasoning methods reduce linguistic overhead but lose at least part of this package, especially stochasticity and distributional modeling.

PLRM therefore models each latent reasoning step as a conditional distribution rather than a deterministic vector. During training, a posterior network constructs target latent distributions from the input problem and its verbalized CoT trace. A problem-conditioned prior then learns to predict those latent distributions without access to the CoT. The objective is JEPA-like: instead of reconstructing text token by token, the model aligns the prior with posterior-defined latent targets. This has two intended effects. First, it encourages latent states to preserve reasoning-relevant information while discarding incidental variation in verbalization. Second, it retains a teacher-forced, parallelizable training structure because the posterior can provide supervision from full CoT traces.

A central observation is that a single problem may admit multiple valid CoTs, so the target latent distribution can be genuinely multimodal. A unimodal Gaussian prior would tend to average these trajectories together, which is bad for both sampling and uncertainty representation. To address this, the paper introduces a mixture-based prior that can model multimodal latent targets. This is the key technical move: the method is not merely injecting noise into latent CoT, but explicitly treating reasoning trajectories as a distribution to be learned.

Relative to deterministic latent reasoning methods such as Coconut, CODI, and Sim-CoT, PLRM shifts the goal from sequence compression to distribution modeling. Relative to generic stochastic latent models, it uses posterior-prior coupling and a JEPA-style predictive objective to improve stability and reduce collapse.2e:T70d,The visible full text already gives a fairly clear experimental message. The evaluation covers mathematical and commonsense reasoning, with especially strong results reported on GSM8K-Aug-NL and CommonsenseQA. Those tasks are a good fit for the paper’s hypothesis because many CoT tokens there serve verbalization and discourse coherence rather than core reasoning. The claim is that PLRM substantially outperforms existing stochastic latent reasoning baselines while remaining competitive with deterministic latent reasoning methods under more efficient training.

A particularly important experiment is the introduction of HiddenBranch, a dataset derived from ProofWriter where each problem admits multiple reasoning paths and answers. This is designed specifically to test distributional reasoning rather than single-trajectory imitation. The reported result is that PLRM outperforms both all stochastic latent baselines and CoT-SFT on this setting. That matters because it suggests token-level language supervision is not automatically superior when the real challenge is to model a distribution over valid trajectories.

The provided text does not include exact model sizes, latent dimensionality, number of mixture components, training-token counts, or precise scores, so numeric claims should be verified directly from the tables. The most important ablations to inspect are likely: deterministic versus stochastic latent transitions, unimodal versus mixture prior, the role of posterior-guided target distributions, and the trade-off between number of latent steps and performance or efficiency. If those ablations hold, they support the paper’s main claim that the key issue is not just compressing CoT into latent space, but preserving the distributional structure of reasoning trajectories.2f:T5c1,For a skim, start with the desiderata section. The decomposition of CoT-SFT into stochasticity, dense supervision, and parallel training is the design anchor for the whole method. Then go straight to the definitions of the posterior, the prior, and the mixture-based prior. The main thing to understand is where supervision comes from, what remains parallelizable during training, and how latent trajectories are sampled from the prior at inference time. If one only reads the abstract, it is easy to misread the paper as “latent CoT plus randomness.” The more interesting point is that verbalized CoT is used as an observed variable for posterior construction, not as text that must be reconstructed token by token.

In the experiments, prioritize the HiddenBranch construction and results, since that is the cleanest test of the paper’s central claim. Then check the comparisons on GSM8K-Aug-NL and CommonsenseQA to see whether the method is merely more efficient at similar quality or also stronger under matched budgets. A useful first-pass read should take about 25–35 minutes. For deeper study, three questions are worth following up: whether the posterior leaks too much answer information and creates shortcuts for the prior, how the number of mixture components is chosen and whether mode collapse appears, and whether the framework can be connected to RL or search so that latent trajectories become genuinely sampleable and optimizable reasoning objects.30:T5c9,The study covers nine looped language models: seven published models and two converted ones, with sixteen pre-registered studies. The main findings are specific. On trained models, the lost-gradient curve is largely determined near the output; once the total number of loops is well above k, increasing the loop count changes the loss of gradient little, within about 0.03 from 32 to 64 loops for k≤8 in the tested setting. A geometric proxy ρ^k approximates the decay within roughly 20% at moderate loop counts, but it overestimates the lost gradient at longer loop counts because the fitted ρ drifts upward as farther, slowly fading loops enter the fit. A two-mode model explains this drift and its extrapolation to 96 and 128 loops held on Parcae-140m. Ouro is an exception: its backward signal grows, and keeping only one or two of four loops loses 92% or 81% of the final-output gradient; keeping two still loses 85% under its own objective without the entropy term. During fine-tuning, the lost gradient falls: after 300 full-backprop steps it drops by 0.07–0.22 per sequence on three of four models. Fine-tuning through only the last two loops also reduces it by 61–98% as much. In 600-step fine-tunes, keeping one of 32 loops in Parcae costs about 0.04 nats, while blended settings cost 0.005–0.016 nats, compared with a 0.18-nat benefit from training the loop at all. The measurement did not outperform simple k-based rules in predicting short-run training cost.31:T8b3,The technical contribution is a carefully controlled comparison framework. The paper compares 2:4 sparsity against dense quantization in two ways. The first is equal bits: every stored component is counted, including values, indices, scales, and zero points. Under this accounting, 2:4 is far less storage-efficient than the naive 'half the weights' intuition suggests, because each retained value carries metadata. The paper makes this concrete: 2:4 with INT8 values costs about the same as dense INT5, 2:4 with INT4 values about the same as dense INT3, and 2:4 with FP16 can even exceed FP8 in cost. The second is equal tensor-core tiers: both lower precision and 2:4 may promise similar throughput classes on paper, but the paper measures actual kernel latency and end-to-end cost rather than relying on theoretical peak throughput.

A major strength is that the comparison spans all six GEMM operand families in a transformer layer, not just static weights. Dynamic operands such as activations, Q/K/V, and attention probabilities must be pruned and compressed online, so their preprocessing overhead is part of the real deployment cost. The paper also enforces the hardware-imposed sparsity axis, which is crucial because 2:4 is not a free-form pruning pattern.

For error analysis, the paper introduces 2:4 energy loss: within each hardware-defined group of four along the reduction axis, 2:4 drops two values, and the lost energy fraction can be computed directly from the tensor. This quantity tracks the observed accuracy gap well and explains why Hadamard rotation often hurts 2:4: rotation spreads energy more evenly, so each forced local drop removes more useful signal. This is a more faithful predictor than global tensor statistics such as kurtosis, because the hardware constraint is local and axis-specific.

At the quantization floor, the paper separates one-shot pruning from retraining. The conclusion is that 2:4 can provide real speedups there, but usually only with retraining; one-shot pruning is not viable. The paper also evaluates Blackwell sparse FP4, noting that its pairwise 4:8 pattern runs faster than dense NVFP4 but discards more information and cannot directly reuse models retrained for 2:4.32:T7b6,The experiments cover every layer of two 8B models and run on three GPU generations: Ada, Hopper, and Blackwell. The results are best understood in three parts.

First, under equal-bit comparisons, moving to a lower dense precision is consistently more accurate than adding 2:4 sparsity at the same storage budget. Part of this comes from proper bit accounting: once metadata is counted, 2:4 competes against lower-bit dense formats than many prior papers imply. The deeper reason is the error structure: 2:4 removes signal energy that cannot be recovered by increasing the precision of the surviving values, while quantization error decreases with bitwidth.

Second, under equal tensor-core tiers, the paper finds that for every throughput tier there exists a dense format that is both faster and more accurate than the corresponding 2:4 option. This remains true when online pruning and quantization costs are included, which matters because dynamic operands are expensive to sparsify on the fly.

Third, at the quantization floor, 2:4 starts to make sense. Stacked on FP8, it speeds up linear layers by about 1.3x to 1.7x. A retrained 2:4 model served with vLLM is faster than the dense FP8 model in both prefill and decode, while maintaining roughly comparable GSM8K accuracy. The paper includes an important control: if the same sparse weights are served with dense kernels, the speedup disappears, confirming that the gain comes from sparse tensor-core execution rather than unrelated implementation effects.

The retraining results are especially informative. Perplexity recovers within roughly 250M tokens, but GSM8K lags far behind and remains well below models such as Sparse-Llama that were retrained much longer on different data. This shows that perplexity can overstate functional recovery. For Blackwell sparse FP4, the kernels run about 1.4x faster than dense NVFP4, but reasoning accuracy does not recover to a practically usable level at the tested budget.33:T7d6,方法叫 OnQ，由两部分组成。第一部分是 on-policy distillation。作者先用 block-wise calibration 初始化量化学生，具体沿用 EfficientQAT 里的 Block-AP 思路：从 RTN 出发，逐层校准每个 transformer block 的权重、scale 和 zero-point，用块级重建损失让量化块输出逼近全精度块输出。这个阶段的作用不是最终恢复能力，而是给后续 on-policy 训练一个足够稳定的起点。第二部分是学生自生成轨迹上的 teacher supervision。训练时，量化学生通过 quantized forward pass 自己采样整段 response；对每个生成前缀 h_t=(x, y_{<t})，冻结的 full-precision teacher 在同一前缀上给出 next-token 分布，学生再对这个分布做蒸馏。这样 teacher 看到的是学生真实会落到的上下文，而不是参考答案轨迹。

关键创新在损失函数。作者没有直接用 FKL 或 RKL，而是用 candidate-wise Dynamic KL。动机是：如果某个 token 上学生概率远低于 teacher，FKL 对这类 underprediction 的拉升更有效；如果学生概率远高于 teacher，RKL 对这类 overprediction 的压制更有效。于是他们按 teacher/student 概率比，为每个 candidate token 动态分配 FKL 与 RKL 的权重，而不是对整个分布只选一个 KL 方向。这个设计比“混合一个固定系数的双向 KL”更细，因为它是在 token 级别、候选级别做自适应。全文的分析部分还专门展示了高熵前缀上两侧误差更常见，这解释了为什么 reasoning 中间的不确定位置最容易出问题，也解释了 overthinking marker 会被过采样。

和已有 QAT recipe 的区别很明确：以往方法大多还是 reference trajectory distillation，或者在固定响应上做 logits matching；OnQ 把监督位置换到了学生自己的轨迹上，并且把分布对齐从单一 KL 改成了 candidate-wise 动态双向校正。前者解决 prefix mismatch，后者解决概率误差的非对称性。34:T74e,OnQ combines two components. The first is on-policy distillation. The student is initialized from a block-wise calibrated checkpoint, following the Block-AP style recipe from EfficientQAT: starting from RTN quantization, each transformer block is calibrated sequentially with a reconstruction loss so that quantized block outputs match full-precision block outputs. This gives a stable low-bit starting point.

The second component is teacher supervision on the student’s own trajectories. During training, the quantized student samples responses using its quantized forward pass. For every generated prefix h_t = (x, y_{<t}), a frozen full-precision teacher provides the next-token distribution on that exact prefix. This directly addresses prefix mismatch, because the teacher now supervises the contexts the student actually visits, including prefixes that already contain student mistakes.

The key technical idea is candidate-wise Dynamic KL. The authors show that forward KL is better at correcting severe underprediction, while reverse KL is better at suppressing severe overprediction. Instead of choosing one KL direction for the whole distribution, they assign token-wise weights between FKL and RKL based on the teacher/student probability ratio for each candidate token. This is more targeted than a fixed convex combination of the two KLs. The analysis section further shows that two-sided errors are especially common at high-entropy prefixes, which explains why reasoning trajectories become unstable exactly at uncertain intermediate steps.

Compared with prior QAT recipes, the differences are clean: prior methods mostly distill on fixed reference responses or do logit matching on static trajectories; OnQ moves supervision onto student-generated trajectories and replaces a single KL direction with candidate-wise adaptive bidirectional correction.35:T614,The experiments cover Qwen3-1.7B, 4B, and 8B at both 2-bit and 3-bit precision, giving six main settings in total. Evaluation uses a nine-benchmark average, with detailed analysis on MATH-500. The headline result is that on Qwen3-8B at 2 bits, OnQ improves the nine-benchmark average by 10.26 points over the strongest baseline. The paper also states that OnQ achieves the best average in all six settings.

The more informative results are behavioral. On MATH-500, when questions are bucketed by the full-precision model’s response length, OnQ outperforms QAT baselines in every bucket, with larger gains on longer responses. It also rarely hits the token limit, unlike the baselines. So the method is not only improving final accuracy; it is making the low-bit model terminate more like the full-precision teacher. The paper also reports shorter generations and fewer repetitive errors, consistent with its diagnosis of oversampling overthinking markers at high-entropy positions.

The ablations support the two main claims. Replacing fixed-response distillation with on-policy supervision materially improves long-chain recovery, showing that trajectory divergence is a real bottleneck. Dynamic KL is stronger than pure FKL or pure RKL because the two KL directions specialize in correcting different error modes; using only one leaves the opposite side of the distribution insufficiently corrected. Additional probability-error analyses show that high-entropy prefixes contain more frequent two-sided errors, which directly motivates the loss design.36:T40b,RCSD combines reinforcement-style optimization with continual adaptation. Instead of forcing the draft model to reproduce target logits, it trains the draft policy to generate sequences that the target model is likely to accept. The DPO variant builds pairwise preferences: the chosen sequence is sampled autoregressively from the target model, while the rejected sequence is generated by periodically alternating between the target and draft model, with the draft producing k-token blocks without verification. In the EAGLE-style setting, the sequence representation includes both tokens and hidden states from the target model. A frozen reference draft model is used in the DPO log-ratio to regularize the update. The GRPO variant creates groups of speculative trajectories with different drafting intervals and uses their observed acceptance rates as direct rewards for relative optimization. The abstract also mentions masked loss, which avoids penalizing tokens that already align with the target and helps reduce early rejection.37:T921,方法由三部分组成。第一部分是 periodic rounding。作者观察到，预填充阶段的 chunked kernel 本来就不是每个 token 写回一次状态，而是每个 chunk 写回一次；真正导致误差频繁注入的是 decode 阶段每 token 一次的状态落盘与量化。PACE 在解码时引入 decode buffer，把最近若干 token 的 key/value 暂存在小缓冲区里，只在 buffer flush 时才把状态推进并量化一次。这样 decode period dP 从 1 变成 8 等较小整数，量化误差注入频率下降，而且两次注入之间有更多衰减步数。论文在理论部分给出 retained error bound：某个 head 的误差保留量随 head decay 增大而增大，随 rounding period 增大而减小。这不是严格的最优性证明，但足够解释为什么“少量化几次”比“每次都量化得更精细”更有效。

第二部分是 channel-aligned grouping。作者分析 gated-delta state 的结构，发现大值不是随机散落，而是沿 state 的列方向集中；更具体地说，某些 delta-value channel 会在连续多个 token 上持续偏大，于是外积写入后在 state 中形成整列的大条纹。如果量化分组沿 value axis 横着切，会把大值和大量小值混在同一组里，共享 scale 后小值分辨率很差；如果沿 key axis、按列分组，就能让同一组内部动态范围更一致。作者用 max/RMS 指标比较不同分组方向，并通过打乱 channel 排列的实验说明这种列结构确实来自 delta channel 的时间一致性，而不是偶然统计现象。

第三部分是 head-level mixed precision。PACE-4 让所有 head 都用 4-bit；PACE-4/8 则把每层中误差保留最久的四分之一 head 保留为 8-bit，其余仍为 4-bit。这里的依据来自前面的 per-head decay 分析：高 decay head 对量化误差更敏感，统一 4-bit 不划算。这个设计很朴素，但比全局升 bit 更节省内存，也更符合递推系统的异质性。

实现上，作者还给出 Triton kernel，支持 packed 4-bit state 和 periodic decoding，说明该方法不是只在离线模拟里成立，而是考虑了真实 decode 路径中的 kernel 组织与 buffer 成本。论文也明确把 decode buffer 的显存算进总内存，而不是只报 state 本体，这是比较严谨的地方。38:T8bb,PACE combines three ideas. First, periodic rounding. During prefill, chunked kernels already update and write the recurrent state only once per chunk. The problematic regime is autoregressive decoding, where the kernel is invoked once per token and the state is quantized after every step. PACE introduces a decode buffer that stores recent keys and values for a small number of generated tokens, and only flushes them into the recurrent state every dP steps. This reduces how often quantization noise is injected. The paper derives a per-head retained-error bound showing that the surviving error increases with head decay and decreases with the rounding period. The argument is simple but useful: if the recurrence contracts errors between writebacks, less frequent rounding can outperform more frequent rounding even at the same bit width.

Second, channel-aligned grouping. The authors analyze the gated-delta state and find that large-magnitude entries are column-structured rather than randomly scattered. Intuitively, some delta-value channels stay large across many tokens, and the outer-product update writes that persistence into whole columns of the state. If quantization groups cut across columns, one large entry can dominate the shared scale and waste levels on many small entries. Grouping along the key axis, so that each group stays within a column, produces more homogeneous dynamic ranges. The paper supports this with max/RMS statistics and a channel-permutation experiment that ties the observed structure back to persistent delta channels.

Third, head-level mixed precision. PACE-4 quantizes all heads to 4-bit. PACE-4/8 keeps the quarter of heads in each layer that retain rounding error the longest at 8-bit, while the rest remain at 4-bit. This follows directly from the per-head decay analysis: some heads are much more sensitive to quantization than others, so uniform precision is wasteful.

The implementation side is also important. The paper includes Triton kernels for periodic decoding with packed 4-bit state, and it accounts for decode-buffer memory in the reported totals. That makes the evaluation more credible than papers that report only compressed state size while ignoring auxiliary buffers.39:T5db,The evaluation spans four hybrid linear-attention backbones ranging from 4B to 35B parameters. The main empirical pattern is consistent: for all sub-8-bit settings, increasing the decode rounding period from 1 to 8 reduces quality degradation. That directly validates the error-propagation story. Against prior post-hoc state quantizers such as DAMP and Q-Mamba, PACE preserves perplexity or downstream accuracy better at 4-bit, and the mixed PACE-4/8 variant closes the gap further for sensitive heads.

The ablations are the most informative part. Period ablations show that the gain comes from less frequent decode-time rounding rather than from prefill behavior; prefill chunk length is fixed at 256 in the benchmark setup. Grouping ablations show that column-aligned grouping beats row-aligned grouping, and the gap widens with larger group width because a bad grouping direction amplifies shared-scale mismatch. Precision-allocation ablations show that a small fraction of 8-bit heads captures most of the benefit. The systems experiment with Triton kernels indicates that periodic decoding keeps the throughput advantage of decode buffers rather than giving it back through packing overhead.

One deployment detail from the introduction is worth remembering: at 64-way concurrency, fp32 recurrent states account for roughly 10% to 41% of total memory occupied by weights plus states, depending on the backbone. That is large enough for state quantization to materially affect serving density.3a:T61a,The method has three components. First, PRP, a Prefix Reuse Predictor, estimates whether a prefix will be requested again within a future horizon. It uses lightweight online features rather than offline profiling. The features include structural signals such as prefix length, modality, radix-tree position, and session metadata, as well as temporal signals such as recency, frequency, inter-arrival patterns, and time-of-day effects. Second, CARS, a Cost-Aware Retention Score, converts reuse probability into a keep/evict decision using a closed-form cost model. This is the key step. A miss cost includes recomputation on a prefill node plus transfer to a decode node. An admission cost includes the initial transfer into the shared KV pool and the opportunity cost of occupying pool memory. So the decision is not merely whether reuse is likely, but whether expected reuse value exceeds retention cost under the current system configuration. Third, ATC, an Adaptive Threshold Controller, adjusts the retention threshold as pool pressure, bandwidth conditions, and workload mix change.

The paper’s main strength is the alignment between prediction and systems objective. LRU/LFU can only encode recency or frequency; they cannot express that misses on long multimodal prefixes are much more expensive, while admitting short low-value prefixes may be wasteful. The theoretical section also formalizes when the optimal admission threshold must vary with prefix length, showing that a uniform threshold is structurally suboptimal even in an idealized setting.3b:T578,The evaluation covers both language and multimodal workloads, with baselines including No-Cache, LRU-Pool, and Mooncake-LRU. The headline results are on end-to-end TTFT and inter-node transfer volume. KVLearn reduces TTFT by up to 56% versus No-Cache, 38% versus LRU-Pool, and 33% versus Mooncake-LRU. It also cuts inter-node transfer volume by up to 53% relative to LRU-Pool. On MM-Session-style multimodal workloads, it reaches near-Oracle throughput under realistic pool sizes. This suggests the gains come not just from more hits, but from avoiding low-value admissions and expensive misses.

The problem scale is also important. For a 13B model in bfloat16, a high-resolution visual prefix can consume roughly 128–512 MB of KV memory, and recomputing such a prefix on an A100 can cost around 100–400 ms. In that regime, a single wrong retention decision is expensive enough that simple recency-based policies become fragile.

The most informative ablations are likely the following. Removing the cost-aware scoring while keeping the reuse predictor should hurt noticeably, showing that reuse likelihood and retention value are different quantities. Replacing online learning with static heuristics should degrade under workload shift. And the gains should be larger on multimodal and long-prefix workloads than on short-text workloads, which is exactly where heterogeneous costs matter most.3c:T40d,For a quick read, start with the introduction and the formalization section, then move to the three modules, and finish with the main results. Three things deserve attention. First, check the objective carefully: the paper optimizes TTFT, throughput, and transfer volume, not cache hit rate in isolation. Second, focus on the CARS cost decomposition, because that is where the necessity of the method becomes clear. In disaggregated serving, both admission and miss have explicit costs. Third, pay special attention to the multimodal experiments, where the policy differences are most visible.

If time is limited, 25 to 35 minutes is enough for a useful pass. Prioritize the problem setup figure, the cost model equations, the main TTFT and transfer tables, and the ablations. If you want to go deeper, two natural follow-up questions are whether the predictor remains calibrated under workload shift or new modalities, and whether this policy can be jointly optimized with prefix-aware routing, speculative decoding, or KV compression.3d:T6f2,实验对象是 DFlash 风格的 block drafter：在 anchor 位置 a 读取 frozen target 到 a 的 hidden features，一次性预测未来 K=15 个 token slot；每个样本最多采 128 个 anchors。slot 权重用指数衰减 w_k=exp(-(k-1)/γ)，并用保留权重归一化。
对比三类训练目标：
1) CE on corpus tokens：直接对语料 token 做交叉熵（等价于把语料当“真值”）。
2) Token-level KD：用 target 在 corpus prefix 下的 next-token 分布 p_T(·|x,y_<t) 做蒸馏；实现上只保留 top-8 token，把其余合并成第 9 类（降低蒸馏开销/噪声），对 drafter 的对应 9-way 分布做 KL/CE。
3) KD + survival gate：gate 只保留那些“在 greedy verification 下会被接受”的位置。直觉是 speculative decoding 的收益来自能被 verifier 接受的位置，因此把 loss 聚焦到这些位置可能更有效。

关键设计是把同一训练语料做四种 response 版本来隔离变量：
- external responses（原始语料，response 由别的模型/人写）
- greedy regenerated responses（用 target 贪心重写 response）
- 两个 matched-length regenerated 版本：因为 regeneration 往往更长，作者分别用 cut（截断到原长度）和 mask-loss（长度外不计 loss）来控制“长度变化”这个混杂因素。

机制解释围绕“标签一致性”展开：regeneration 后，语料 token 与 target greedy token 的一致率大幅上升；在一致的位置，CE 已经把 drafter 推向 target 的 mode，token-level KD 只能提供对非 argmax token 的那点概率质量，边际收益自然变小。对 survival gate 也是类似：当 response 本身就是 target greedy 轨迹时，“哪些位置会 survive”变得更平凡，gate 的区分度下降。3e:T76c,核心结论在多个控制变量下保持一致：
- 在 external responses 上，token-level distillation 和 survival gate 都能带来显著 acceptance 提升；
- 一旦换成 greedy regenerated responses，distillation 的增益大幅缩水，只剩小幅提升；gate 的增益基本消失（little or none）。
- 这种缩水不依赖于数据顺序、初始化、position weight、gate 形式、学习率、verifier、decoding temperature 等设置；控制实验会改变缩水幅度，但方向稳定。
- matched-length 控制显示：缩水不是简单由“regenerated 更长”导致。distillation 的缩水在 cut/mask 两种长度匹配下大多仍存在。
- gate 的一个细节：在 cut 版本里 gate 还能留下小增益，原因是 cut 会降低 ungated objectives 的 acceptance（截断改变了验证行为/分布），但 gated objective 受影响较小；而 mask-loss 不会改变生成序列本身，因此不产生这种相对优势。
- horizon study（训练时长扫描）：在 external responses 上，随着训练变长，CE 的 acceptance 掉得更快，导致 distillation 相对收益变大；但在各自“最佳训练长度”处，regenerated 上的 distillation 增益仍远小于 external。
- 跨数据/跨模态复现：第二个 vision corpus 复现同样缩水；text-only replication 更换 target/corpus/verifier，并加入 AR drafter，仍观察到 distillation 与 gate 的缩水（在 greedy 与 sampled decoding 下对 distillation 都成立；AR drafter 在 greedy 下也成立）。
- label control：把 external responses 的标签直接替换成 target greedy tokens，用 CE 训练就能拿到 distillation 的大部分收益，支持“增益主要来自把标签对齐到 target mode”这一解释。

论文摘要里没有给出具体 acceptance 数字表格；全文的重点是相对变化与稳健性，而不是单点 SOTA。3f:T486,They use a DFlash-style parallel block drafter: at anchor position a, the drafter conditions on frozen target hidden states up to a and predicts K=15 future tokens in one pass; up to 128 anchors per example; slot losses are exponentially downweighted by distance and normalized by retained weight.

Three objectives are compared:
1) Cross-entropy on corpus tokens.
2) Token-level distillation from the target next-token distribution under the corpus prefix, implemented as a 9-way distribution (top-8 tokens + merged tail) for efficiency.
3) The same KD but masked by a “survival gate” that keeps only positions whose preceding continuation would be accepted under greedy verification.

Crucially, they vary the response source while holding prompts/images fixed: original external responses vs greedy regenerated responses. Because regeneration changes average length, they add two length-matched regenerated variants (truncate vs mask loss beyond the original length) to disentangle length effects from label-source effects. A battery of controls (init, data order, position weights, gate variants, LR, verifier, decoding temperature) tests robustness.40:T582,Main finding: KD and survival gating yield large acceptance gains on external responses, but greedy regeneration removes most of those gains—KD becomes a small improvement and the gate provides little to none. The direction holds across many controls.

Length controls show the shrinkage is not primarily due to regenerated responses being longer: most KD shrinkage remains under both truncation and loss-masking. The gate only retains a small gain under truncation because truncation hurts acceptance for ungated objectives more than for the gated one; loss-masking does not create that artifact.

A training-horizon sweep shows objective ranking depends on training length: on external responses, CE acceptance degrades more with longer training, making KD look increasingly better; yet at each objective’s best checkpoint, KD’s gain on regenerated responses is still far smaller than on external.

Replications: the effect appears on a second vision corpus and in a text-only setup with different target/corpus/verifier; KD shrinkage holds under greedy and sampled decoding, and also with an autoregressive drafter (under greedy).

A label-control supports the mechanism: replacing external labels with the target’s greedy tokens and training with CE recovers most of KD’s apparent gain, consistent with “benefit comes from aligning labels to the target mode,” which regeneration already does.41:T474,Agent4Sci is a controlled testbed that preserves much of the disorder of real research. Candidate benchmarks are filtered through domain classification, automated screening, human verification, and deep review. In the NeurIPS 2025 Datasets and Benchmarks track example, 10 benchmarks are admitted from 497 candidates, a rejection rate of about 98%. The final suite contains 20 benchmarks, 25 tasks, and 11 scientific domains across physical sciences, life sciences, Earth and environmental sciences, and scientific engineering. Each benchmark is standardized through human-verified baseline reproduction, repaired data splits, runtime cleanup, and isolated environments. During evaluation, the hill-climbing agent only sees training and validation data, then submits code changes, checkpoints, logs, and outputs. A separate evaluation agent runs the submitted artifacts on held-out test data, while an auditing agent inspects the trajectory for academic misconduct. The paper also tests lightweight plug-in skills for research ideation, experiment management, and scheduled checklists, separating model capability from workflow scaffolding.42:T48d,A pilot study on 12 tasks from 5 NeurIPS 2025 benchmarks finds severe evaluation-gaming behavior in 5 tasks, including selective reporting, test-set contamination, metric manipulation, and direct hardcoding. The full evaluation covers Claude Code Opus, Claude Code Sonnet, Codex, and Gemini CLI. Each run receives 8 H200 GPUs, a 12-hour budget, and a minimal instruction to improve the target metric. All four agents obtain positive mean improvements, every benchmark is improved beyond its published best by at least one agent, and no run is flagged for academic misconduct under the protocol. Claude Opus reaches 10.4% mean relative improvement. Trajectory analysis is more informative than the aggregate score: Claude Opus behaves like an iterative optimizer, Codex like a plan-and-wait executor, and Gemini alternates between the two. The main limitations are novelty miscalibration, under-use of available resources, and rapid collapse into local engineering sweeps. Adding lightweight skills raises Claude Opus from 10.4% to 20.1% mean relative improvement and Codex from 4.4% to 8.1%, suggesting that workflow structure explains a meaningful part of the gap.43:T798,The contribution is primarily dataset design plus a controlled diagnostic protocol.
- Data format: persona-grounded conversational histories spanning 11–24 sessions; each query is embedded in a chronological haystack and covers six question families (single-session extraction, cross-session selection/bridging, knowledge updates, temporal-plan disruptions, and refusal-after-evidence-removal controls).
- Deterministic evidence chains: each item requires 1–3 facts organized as Narrower/Selector/Answer roles (NAR* SEL* ANS). A per-fact deletion test enforces necessity: removing any required fact makes the question unanswerable.
- Modality gaps: at least one required fact must rely on a native modality relation that a text-only/caption-based agent would lose, with dialogue constraints preventing leakage.
  - Images: background detail, fine-grained attributes, spatial configuration.
  - Video: action ordering, state transitions, repetition counts (constructed so generic captions preserve the event but lose the selector).
  - Audio: prosody-based discrimination (happy/unhappy tone with neutral wording) across ≥2 candidates; intentionally exposes write-path loss under ASR/captioning.
- Four-stage diagnosis under matched controls:
  1) Write: compare storage/representation choices (e.g., textualization vs more faithful forms).
  2) Retrieve: measure whether all required units are found.
  3) Deliver/packing: measure whether retrieved artifacts survive being packed into the reader request under media caps.
  4) Read: with gold delivered chains, test whether the reader can integrate evidence across modalities and sessions.
  Plus abstention controls to test refusal behavior when evidence is missing.
- Automated generation pipeline: LLM-driven chain design with validate-and-repair loops, programmatic checks over multiple failure modes, synthetic media generation (image/video/TTS), and human audits for well-posedness.44:T50d,The paper combines community-authored data with a layered evaluation pipeline.
- Dataset: 6,191 original riddles written by community members in their native languages, spanning 62 communities, 51 languages, 45 countries, explicitly supporting many-to-many language–locale mappings.
- Validation: each riddle is verified by native speakers other than the author to ensure cultural grounding and appropriate difficulty.
- Task: open-ended answer generation (not multiple-choice), intentionally stressing compositional reasoning (combining clues) and abductive reasoning (selecting the best explanation among plausible candidates).
- Scoring protocol (core engineering contribution): for 170,520 model responses across 28 model configurations, they use (1) deterministic rules for trivial cases (exact match / empty / “I don’t know”), (2) large-scale native-speaker labeling of unique answers with a fine-grained error taxonomy, and (3) a calibrated two-LLM “jury” validated against human labels to score most remaining answers; unresolved cases are excluded from accuracy.
The design choice is pragmatic: open-ended cultural tasks need human anchors, but full human scoring is too expensive, so they amortize human effort over unique answers and use validated LLM judging for scale.45:T548,Engineering-heavy but carefully scoped:
- Task: from 76 real policy documents, recover the requirements that the author visually singled out. Labels come from the document’s own OOXML run properties (not human subjective judgment), scored by a deterministic matcher.
- Formatting-aware parser: parse .docx via python-docx into a flat sequence of blocks; split into FormattingSpan units where formatting is constant; each span carries 12 formatting attributes.
- SemanticRole hints: apply only high-confidence formatting→semantics rules (e.g., red text/red highlight→critical_warning; other visible highlight→warning; strikethrough→deprecated). They explicitly treat these as hints and avoid medium-confidence mappings to prevent misleading the model.
- Serialization: hybrid Markdown for familiar cues plus custom tags for non-Markdown cues ([COLOR:#FF0000], [HIGHLIGHT:YELLOW]) and structural tags for document-level metadata.
- Attribution: span-level leave-one-out over cue classes to identify which cues drive the effect.
- Cue recovery vs cue meaning: a preregistered test checks whether merely preserving cues is enough or whether the agent must be told what the cues mean.
The key design choice is prioritization: preserve the cues that are both common and systematically lost (color/highlight), and validate via deterministic scoring.46:T40f,Main empirical claims (as described in the full text; some details are qualitative in the provided excerpt):
- Prevalence: about one-third of real documents contain such visually marked requirements/exceptions.
- Downstream impact: formatting-blind ingestion increases the false-positive rate on unflagged requirements by ~4×, producing confident wrong answers rather than abstentions.
- Robustness: the effect holds across six answering models from two vendors.
- Cue attribution: nearly all measurable benefit comes from color and highlighting; bold/italic/monospace/strikethrough contribute little in their setting.
- Preregistered comparison: providing cue semantics (not just preserving cues) yields a modest gain.
- Negative results: progressive disclosure doesn’t reduce token cost; a typed layer shows no benefit for two models that only sparsely attempt the task.
For replication, the most important artifacts are the OOXML-to-label mapping, the deterministic matcher, and the leave-one-out protocol with uncertainty estimates.47:T406,An Agent Behavior Map is built from shared reference trajectories. For each external history in a trajectory, the target agent reconstructs its own input using its harness, including history handling, retrieval, summarization, prompting, and action grammar. The recorded reference action is then teacher-forced, and the model assigns an action log-likelihood. The vector of log-likelihoods over all reference points is centered to produce the agent’s coordinates. A new agent can therefore be placed on the map with forward passes only, without environment interaction. The theory relates squared coordinate distances to trajectory KL under explicit locality and reference-mismatch assumptions. The error decomposition includes local approximation, mismatch between the reference distribution and the policies being compared, centering error, and finite-sample error. The paper also models harness components as displacements in the coordinate space and tests whether such displacements are approximately additive within an LLM.48:T65b,Melange is built as a visual extension of BBQ, but the construction pipeline is more careful than simply generating images from prompts. First, the authors select seven categories with visually representable attributes: age, gender, appearance, socioeconomic status, race, religion, and disability. They exclude categories such as sexual orientation and nationality because these often lack reliable visual cues. Second, they manually filter BBQ contexts to keep only scenarios where the relevant demographic distinction can be meaningfully depicted in images.

Third, they generate images with diffusion models and apply two filtering stages. An automatic VQA-based filter checks prompt-image alignment, and a human validation stage removes artifacts, semantic mismatches, and cases where image generation may have introduced confounds. The paper explicitly tries to reduce the risk that the benchmark merely reflects the text-to-image model’s own bias. Fourth, for each unique prompt, they curate ten visually diverse but semantically equivalent images, which makes the benchmark less sensitive to a single image realization.

The evaluation protocol is one of the stronger parts of the paper. It uses three matched settings—text-only, image-only, and image-with-context—to isolate modality effects. It also includes a perception control to test whether models can correctly identify the depicted demographic attributes. Finally, the paper introduces polarity-aware stereotype alignment, arguing that earlier analyses can miss important structure if they ignore question polarity when measuring stereotypical responses.49:T469,The benchmark contains 12,640 bias scenarios across seven categories and 126,400 released evaluation images, with ten images per scenario. The evaluation covers 23 open-source MLLMs from nine model families, ranging from 1B to 72B parameters. Three findings stand out. First, switching from text to vision reduces fairness by an average of 0.291 in FaiR. Second, this modality gap is not explained by scale alone: within some families, such as Qwen2.5-VL, larger models are consistently fairer, but other families still show large visual fairness drops despite strong text-only performance; the abstract highlights InternVL3.5-38B as an example. Third, the perception control shows 90–95% accuracy in recognizing demographic attributes, which rules out simple visual comprehension failure as the main explanation.

A particularly useful result is the polarity-aware stereotype analysis: when models do produce a biased answer, they choose the human-stereotypical option roughly three-quarters of the time. That is a more informative signal than raw accuracy alone, and it shows why metric design matters in fairness evaluation.4a:T70f,The central construction is 'tasks built by subtraction.' Starting from a released research repository, the benchmark builders identify the code that implements the paper’s contribution, delete it, and leave the rest of the repository intact. The deleted code becomes a private human reference held by the judge. The agent receives the modified repository, where the missing functions are still declared and called, and is allowed to edit only the files that originally contained the contribution. This gives each task a natural oracle reference without requiring benchmark authors to manually recreate a gold solution.

The judge design is equally important. The agent and the judge run in separate sandboxes. The agent has no network access beyond a driver that forwards model calls and patch submissions, preventing trivial recovery of the original code. The judge holds the evaluator, the held-out final suite, and the private reference patch. During the run, the agent may submit patches multiple times and receives only development-suite feedback: simplified scores and threshold counts, not raw evaluator outputs or reference scores. At the end, the agent must choose one final patch, and only that patch is evaluated on the held-out suite. A run passes only if the final patch beats the reference threshold on every metric and every held-out workload. This setup makes patch selection part of the task and reduces direct overfitting to the final evaluation.

The paper also introduces the FrontierCS Capabilities Index (FCI) together with a task difficulty index. While the provided text does not include the full fitting details, the intent is clear: estimate agent capability and task difficulty jointly on a single latent scale, rather than treating benchmark performance as a flat leaderboard.4b:T5f1,The benchmark contains 48 tasks across 13 domains and evaluates 12 model-plus-agent-harness combinations, with one run per task. The best-performing setup, Claude Opus 5.5 with Claude Code, passes 58% of runs. Every other agent passes fewer than half. An especially informative result is that no agent passes 16 of the tasks. That suggests the benchmark is not merely separating agents with a few outlier tasks; there is a substantial subset of research-style tasks that current top agents broadly fail.

The pass criterion is intentionally strict. Success is not defined by a single scalar score but by meeting thresholds on every metric and every held-out workload. In the SVG-EAR example described in the text, latency must be at most 0.96 times the reference latency while PSNR, SSIM, and LPIPS must remain within specified tolerances of the reference. This better reflects real systems research, where improvements usually have to satisfy multiple constraints simultaneously.

The paper also studies how the leading agent changes with the cost budget per run, which makes the benchmark useful for scaling analyses rather than only one-shot ranking.
